# Loop log

Append only. Newest at the bottom. Each entry is one heading and at most seven more lines, none of which starts with `#`.
`guard` refuses a pull request that changes or removes a line of this file, or that adds any heading but `## <UTC time> <unit>`
followed by START, PASS, FAIL, BLOCKED or SPLIT, unless the maintainer signed it.

| Heading | Written by | Meaning |
|---------|------------|---------|
| `## <UTC time> <unit> START mode=cloud` | the loop | A run has claimed the unit. It is not a result |
| `## <UTC time> <unit> PASS mode=<cloud or mac> model=<model> tries=<n>` | the loop | The unit is `[x]`, or `[S]` if it waits for `adopt.sh` |
| `## <UTC time> <unit> FAIL ...` | the loop | One more try is used up. After three, the next run marks the unit `[!]` |
| `## <UTC time> <unit> FAIL ... abandoned run` | the loop | A later run found this one dead. It uses up a try only if the unit's result before it was an abandoned run too |
| `## <UTC time> <unit> BLOCKED ...` | the loop | The unit is `[!]` and waits for the maintainer. `BLOCKED.md` says why, or says `needs the maintainer, see the run's transcript` |
| `## <UTC time> <unit> SPLIT ...` | the loop | The unit was split in `PLAN.md` |
| `## <UTC time> - RESUME` | the maintainer only | Start counting failures afresh |

Write the time as `2026-10-31T10:15Z`. The loop halts when the last six results are all FAIL or BLOCKED. To clear
that halt, fix the cause, append a RESUME line here, and land it with `land.sh`. Then close the `loop-halt` issue.

Harness shape that made it work: Start(ctx, Config{Payload, Workspace, CPUs, MemoryMiB, DiskMiB}) exports a judge-made directory into the guest; suite.Session{Run, Workspace, Guest, Another} replaces the bare Run so a judge can open a second session; Output.Total counts the bytes the host took in beyond the 4096 it keeps. Control applies no cap and exports the directory at its host path.
Blocked by SPEC section 7, not by the tests: the test-code budget is 1.5 x product + 2000 lines, product code is 32 lines, so everything under escape/ may hold 2048 lines; main holds 1860 and this unit brings 2729. Nothing in escape/ can go without losing a T2-proven test, and no cut of 188 lines inside the new files was found.

## 2026-11-01T17:37Z U-100 START mode=cloud

## 2026-11-01T18:10Z U-100 PASS mode=cloud model=opus tries=0
Added spikes/libkrun (654 lines Go, notes.md 140 lines) and two direct dependencies (purego v0.11.2, x/sys v0.49.0). The test skips without /dev/kvm; on the hosted runner it downloads libkrun 1.19.7 (crates.io crate) and libkrunfw 5.6.2 by pinned digest, builds libkrun with cargo (LTO off, 58 s), loads it through purego in a child process, and boots a static Go guest: virtio-fs shared directory, vsock echo, exit code 7 propagated, Go guest as PID 1 also works.
Measured on PR #20, gate 131 s of 900: boot p50 699 ms (min 676, max 711, n=20), total p50 701 ms, teardown p50 20 ms; P1's hosted target is met with margin. Sizes: guest 3.46 MB static; host child 2.74 MB (nocgo) or 2.65 MB (cgo), both glibc-dynamic ELF; libkrun.so 5.4 MB; libkrunfw.so.5 21.8 MB.
Learned: A6's one static binary does not hold with libkrun on Linux (purego and cgo both need the ELF interpreter, plus two shared libraries beside it); libkrunfw bundles a GPL-2.0 kernel with a source-offer duty; libkrun's default TSI lets the guest reach host loopback, so a backend must disable the implicit vsock (I4); a NULL envp in krun_set_exec copies the host environment onto the guest cmdline (I5); without /dev/kvm krun_start_enter aborts the process instead of returning.
The ADR step of the spike flow is U-103a's job by the plan (ADR-001 compares all three spikes), so no ADR was written here. The proxy of this session refuses GitHub tag archives, so the crate was pinned instead; CI reached both inputs in under a second.
Next: U-101 (Firecracker and Cloud Hypervisor, model sonnet) can copy the test shape, timing definition and t.Logf keys from spikes/libkrun so that U-103a compares like with like; U-102 is env:macos and waits for a Mac run.

## 2026-11-01T18:13Z U-101 START mode=cloud

## 2026-11-01T18:53Z U-101 PASS mode=cloud model=sonnet tries=0
Added spikes/fcch (1424 lines Go, notes.md), stdlib plus x/sys only, no go.mod change: the test downloads Firecracker v1.17.0, Cloud Hypervisor v54.0, Firecracker's CI kernel 6.1.155 (kernel A) and Cloud Hypervisor's 7.2.8 release kernel (kernel B) by pinned digest, installs virtiofsd from the distribution with apt, builds a static Go guest as /init of a newc cpio written in Go, and boots fc (FC, A, raw block share), ch-blk (CH, A, raw block), ch-fs (CH, B, virtio-fs), 1 warm-up plus 20 timed boots each, plus one SIGKILL boot and two cross-kernel boots. Green on the first hosted push (PR #21, commit 26554d0): TestFCCH 89 s, gate 128 s of 900.
Measured (2 vCPU, 1024 MiB): boot_ms p50 fc 194, ch-blk 121, ch-fs 278; total_ms p50 197 / 121 / 280; teardown_ms p50 501 / 8 / 36; libkrun in the same run 543 / 545 (U-100's own run said 699 / 701, so runners differ and only numbers from one gate run compare). Every row is far under P1's hosted 2.5 s.
Learned: both VMMs are static-pie ELF with no interpreter and no NEEDED, driven by exec plus HTTP or flags, so with them A6 holds (one static Go binary plus one VMM binary fetched by digest); only virtiofsd is glibc-dynamic and nobody prebuilds it. Neither VMM carries a guest exit code (vsock is the channel) and both exit 0 on shutdown. The guest sees lo only, host loopback is unreachable (I4), the canary never reached the guest environment or cmdline (I5), orphans=0 after SIGKILL of the VMM (A9), but sockets and the share image stay in the boot directory (I7 cleanup is the supervisor's job). Firecracker has no virtio-fs and appends pci=off and virtio_mmio.device= to the cmdline itself. Neither VMM runs on macOS.
No ADR here, as with U-100: ADR-001 is U-103a's job once U-102 (env:macos) is done. The architect did not write a file; the builder on sonnet needed one CI push.
Next: the only unit left for the cloud is none until H-103, U-102 is env:macos, U-202 (deps:U-001, env:any, boundary) is open and is the first product code, which also widens the test-code budget that blocks U-007 to U-010b. Read the gate's budget line before any escape unit.

Added internal/wire (144 lines product, 215 lines test, stdlib only, no go.mod change): a 6-byte header (version, type, big-endian uint32 length), MaxPayload 64 KiB checked before any allocation, New(Stream, limit) where Stream needs SetReadDeadline and SetWriteDeadline, one per-frame deadline armed at the first byte and covering the rest of the header and the payload, the same for writes, ErrVersion and ErrTooLarge with the value seen, io.ErrUnexpectedEOF on truncation, os.ErrDeadlineExceeded on timeout; after an error that direction stays failed and never resynchronises on guest-chosen bytes. No logging (I10).
Tests: three table tests (19 read, 3 write, 8 net.Pipe time-limit cases at 50 ms) and FuzzReadFrame, whose oracle is re-encode to a prefix of the input, clean EOF only at a frame boundary, sticky error; 20 s and 30 s fuzz runs clean, -race -count=8 on the deadline cases clean. Local gate 5 s; hosted guard and gate-macos green on the unit commit.

Its attack variants are one line in PARKED.md for U-010 and U-208. Review: no findings; one design note parked.
Learned: a raw blocking AF_VSOCK fd wrapped in os.File returns os.ErrNoDeadline, so the guest init (U-203) must open its channel non-blocking for deadlines to work; ReadFrame waits without limit for the first byte, the caller closes the stream to end an idle read. First product code: the test-code limit is now 2264 lines and the gate reports 2094 used, so about 170 lines remain under escape/ plus _test.go until more product lands; U-007 to U-010b stay blocked.

Facts for the parts: legacy knows only the keychain: source (cli.go, exec of a fixed /usr/bin/security with a scrubbed environment and a 2-minute timeout), so the three sources of this unit are new code in the legacy shape; legacy zeroes nothing and holds every value as a string, so the port must keep values as []byte to the end. Split into B-823a (internal/sigstore: parse and verify a Sigstore bundle in its JSON form, messageSignature and DSSE, chain to an embedded trusted-root snapshot, identity, issuer, Rekor SET and integrated time, fuzz target) and B-823 (the per-source verify step, the last part, deps on both). Scout facts: internal/pins holds no signature, DSSE, Rekor or certificate code; Candidates never downloads an artifact body; npm sets no SHA256 (the registry publishes sha512 only, as dist.integrity); a GitHub release without an asset digest leaves SHA256 empty without error. Hand-rolled keyless verification plus the four download-and-digest paths is well over the 400-line limit of PROMPT.md section 8, so no redteam or builder was spent.
Where each pinned upstream signs: the codex GitHub release ships a .sigstore bundle per tarball, identity https://github.com/openai/codex/.github/workflows/rust-release.yml@refs/tags/rust-v<V>, issuer https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com, and it is the only upstream with a signature; npm packages carry at most registry provenance (the attestations endpoint, a DSSE bundle whose subject is the sha512 of the tarball); the vendor manifest and the copilot and goose GitHub releases publish a checksum or asset digest and no signature; no OCI upstream is pinned yet. So B-823 is: download on the host, sha256 against the release digest or the sha512 against the npm SRI string, then the bundle check through internal/sigstore where the pin names an identity, and a refusal where it names one and none is found (B5 never skips silently).
Budget facts for both parts: the gate counts every non-test .go under cmd/bump or internal/pins as the bump tool, 644 of 800, so internal/sigstore lives outside that count and the verify step in internal/pins must fit in about 150 lines or trim sources.go; product 2276 of 15000, test 4428 of 5414, direct dependencies 2 of 15. The standard library covers x509 chains, ECDSA ASN.1 signatures, ed25519, sha256 and sha512 and base64 SRI; it lacks RFC 6962 inclusion proofs, checkpoint notes, SCTs, PGP and minisign, so the accept of B-823a stops at the SET. spikes/channel/keyless.go proves the chain, SAN and issuer checks on a synthetic chain but skips SET, DSSE and time; its test shows how to mint such a chain.
Version facts for B-825: codex tags are rust-v<V> and parseVersion strips only v, and one npm upstream ships only 0.0.<build>-g<commit> versions, which the selector counts as pre-releases; both need a pin-level answer, not a parser change in this unit.

Added spikes/macos (2164 lines Go, notes.md 321 lines) and one direct dependency (Code-Hex/vz v3.8.0, MIT, cgo; 3 of 15). On the maintainer's second account (M2 Max, macOS 27.2) the test builds a static linux/arm64 Go guest as /init of a newc cpio, downloads kernel A (Firecracker CI 6.1.155 aarch64) and kernel B (Cloud Hypervisor 7.2.8 Image-arm64) by pinned digest, and boots seven rows of 1 warm-up plus 20 timed boots plus one SIGKILL boot: vz-blk and vz-fs-kC (Virtualization.framework through vz, in a child signed ad hoc with com.apple.security.virtualization), container (Apple container 1.6.1 running busybox by digest), krun-blk (libkrun 1.19.0 from krunkit 1.3.2's unsigned tarball, loaded through purego, child signed with com.apple.security.hypervisor) and vfkit-blk (vfkit 0.6.4, Red Hat Developer ID). It skips before any download unless kern.hv_support is 1 and codesign exists, so Linux and the macos-15 runner run only the pure table tests.
Measured, total_ms p50 (P1 developer target 1.0 s): krun-blk 46 to 71, vz-blk 232 to 248, vz-fs-kC 206 to 240, vfkit-blk 235 to 269, container 559 to 594; every booting row meets P1. Kernel B does not boot on Virtualization.framework (VZErrorDomain Code=1 before any console output, three release tags tried), so vz-fs and vz-blk-kB fail and the virtio-fs row uses kernel C, the Kata 6.18 kernel that container installs; Cloud Hypervisor's kernel cannot be shared with macOS.
Learned: unsigned children fail at Validate (vz) or VmCreate (libkrun) and ad hoc signing with the entitlement plist suffices, no Apple approval and no root; A6 on macOS means one Mach-O linking only /usr/lib and /System/Library, which vz meets and libkrun (four third-party dylibs plus firmware) and container (bash wrapper, @rpath dylib, launchd agent) do not; A9: a SIGKILL of vzchild, krunchild or vfkit leaves no orphan, a SIGKILL of the container CLI leaves the container running under launchd; loopback unreachable and no env leak in every row; container has no API beyond exec of its CLI over XPC.
Safeguard note: the auto-mode classifier refused the first builder brief because it directed downloading and running third-party binaries (libkrun dylibs, vfkit). The vz and container rows were built first; the maintainer approved the libkrun and vfkit rows in this supervised session, and the builder then finished them. The architect wrote no file, as in U-100 and U-101: ADR-001 is U-103a's.
Gate on the Mac: GATE PASS in 70 s with the spike at 64 s warm; cold run adds about 15 s of downloads and builds. Budgets unchanged: product 3508 of 15000, test 9861 of 10262.
Next: U-103a (ADR-001, spike, env:any, model opus, deps U-100, U-101, U-102) is takeable by the cloud; it should compare U-101's hosted numbers with these only within a machine class, read spikes/macos/notes.md for the kernel B finding and the signing facts, and weigh vz (A6 holds, cgo) against vfkit (exec model, A7 twin of Firecracker) and libkrun (fastest, A6 fails as shipped).

Split into U-201a (pure Go ext4 writer, internal/rootfs), U-201b (OCI pull by digest and layer unpack into that tree) and U-201 (the command, the pin row and the accept demo): the scout found no filesystem-image, tar-unpack or OCI blob code to reuse (internal/pins only lists tags and fetches manifests; internal/artifact downloads files by URL and digest with 512 MiB caps and no OCI branch), so the whole is about 900 product lines plus tests, over the 400-line limit of PROMPT section 8.
Format decided by the kernel configs read this run: Firecracker's CI 6.1 configs (x86_64 and aarch64) enable ext4, xfs, squashfs and overlay but not erofs; Kata's common fs fragment enables ext4, erofs and overlay but not squashfs. ext4 is the only read-only root format every candidate kernel mounts, so the writer is ext4 (4 KiB blocks, extents, no journal, no checksums) and no mkfs runs (A8). The session has e2fsprogs (e2fsck, debugfs) but no squashfs or erofs tools, docker but no skopeo; registry-1.docker.io and ghcr.io answer 401 at /v2/ (reachable, token needed) and quay.io is refused by the session proxy.
Every spike and the U-200 test boot an initramfs cpio with one Go /init and a raw 4 MiB share image written by Go, so no root image exists yet; _legacy/ holds no Dockerfile or guest package list, so the base image (Debian slim or Ubuntu by digest from Docker Hub, multi-arch) is U-201's choice and its pin row the first under pins/, whose on-disk format no code defines yet.
Budget unchanged: product 3878 of 15000, test 10396 of 10817; U-201a must add product lines before tests (about 1.5 test lines per product line). No builder ran, nothing parked, no S14 trigger.
Next: U-201a is the next takeable unit for the cloud. The claim was PR #56, closed by this run; the state change is its own pull request.

Added internal/rootfs (tree.go 230 lines, ext4.go 497 lines; stdlib only: bufio, encoding/binary, errors, fmt, io, math, sort, strings, time; go.mod unchanged; no exec, no os). Tree: NewTree, Put(path, Node{Kind, Mode, UID, GID, Data, Target, Major, Minor}) adds or replaces (Dir, File, Symlink, Char, Block, FIFO; Mode is 07777 only; parents are never created implicitly, ErrNotFound), Link(old, new) shares one *Node, Remove (recursive), Clear(dir) for opaque directories, Lookup returns the live *Node; one optional leading slash, empty, ., .., over-255-byte components and NUL rejected. (*Tree).WriteExt4(w io.Writer, mtime time.Time): 4 KiB blocks, rev 1, 256-byte inodes, features filetype, extents, large_file, dir_nlink; no journal, no checksums, no 64bit, no flex_bg, no sparse_super (every group carries a superblock and descriptor copy), zero UUID, zero mtime means DefaultMtime 2025-01-01; sequential allocation, children sorted by name, inline symlinks under 60 bytes, device numbers new-style in i_block[1], a file split across groups gets several extents and over four a depth-1 tree; the writer adds an empty lost+found 0700 to the root when the tree lacks one, because e2fsck -fn fails without it; limits 256 groups (32 GiB), 65000 links per file, major up to 0xfff and minor up to 0xfffff.
Tests (348 lines): TestDeterministic (same tree twice and after Remove plus re-Put gives identical bytes), TestMtime, TestE2fsck (every kind, a hard link, a 100 KiB file, a symlink over 60 bytes, uid/gid 70000/80000; e2fsck -fn exit 0, 431/432 files 473/473 blocks, then debugfs stat on 12 entries and a byte-exact dump of the 100 KiB file; both skip when the tool is off PATH), TestMultiGroup (192 MiB, 2 groups, e2fsck 14/32 files 49164/49164 blocks), TestTreeOps table. Local gate 12 s with e2fsck 1.47.0 on PATH.
Review: no R2-valid finding. The reviewer also mounted the images with a real kernel (loop, ro) for empty trees, 0/1/4096/4097-byte files, 255-byte names, 59/60/4095-byte symlink targets, 5000 root files, 40000 root dirs, a 530 MiB file over 5 groups with a depth-1 extent tree, and the superblock block count matched the image length each time; the image SHA-256 was identical across four separate processes with a scrubbed environment. Seven notes parked in one PARKED.md line, two of them one-line hardenings for the next unit in this package (refuse mtime past 2038, refuse NUL in a symlink target).

Added internal/rootfs/oci.go (364 lines) and layer.go (186 lines), stdlib only, go.mod unchanged, no exec. Image{Registry, Repository, Digest, OS, Arch} and Pull(ctx, *http.Client, Image, *Tree) (manifestDigest, error): fetches the object by digest, picks the one manifest for os/arch from an OCI index or Docker manifest list, fetches each layer blob by digest in order and streams it through sha256 into the tree; every manifest and blob is checked against the digest it was requested by, and against the descriptor size. Anonymous bearer token on a 401 challenge, https-only realm, token sent only to the challenged registry host; at most 3 https redirects, Authorization dropped on any host change. Caps: manifest or index 64 KiB, layer blob 512 MiB compressed, 2 GiB inflated across all layers (maxRaw), member and link names 4096 bytes. Media types are an allowlist: OCI and Docker v2 index, manifest, tar and tar+gzip; zstd is refused.
applyLayer: names are cleaned and refused if absent, absolute, or holding .. (before Put, because Tree.split strips one leading slash); missing parents are created as 0755 dirs; hard links resolve the linkname against the tree only; symlink targets are stored text, never followed; char, block and FIFO carry major/minor; .wh.<name> removes (missing is a no-op) and .wh..wh..opq clears, neither creates; within one layer whiteouts apply in the order met, a documented deviation from the OCI lower-layers-only rule. Nothing in the package touches the disk.
Tests (oci_test.go 361 lines): httptest TLS registry with a 401 Bearer challenge and token endpoint; TestPullRefusals (manifest body differs from digest, layer differs from descriptor, over cap, zstd, bad digest or repository, redirect to http), TestApplyLayerRefusals (absolute, .., hard link to absolute or .. or missing, write through a symlink, over cap, truncated stream, 1 MiB pax path), TestApplyLayerBomb, TestPullTokenStaysWithRegistry, FuzzLayer. Fuzzing found a quadratic mkParents on a 1 MiB pax path, fixed by the 4096-byte name cap. Also closed the two U-201a parked hardenings: WriteExt4 refuses an mtime past 2038, Tree.validate refuses NUL in a symlink target.
Review: one R2-valid finding (I10, hostile blob server): io.ReadAll plus slices.Clone let a 4 MiB gzip blob holding one 2047 MiB file reach 4.5 GiB RSS under the 2 GiB cap, because the layer is applied before its digest is known. Fixed in one round with make(h.Size) plus io.ReadFull (reviewer measured 2025 MiB peak); the redundant inflated-stream drain in layer() went too. Four notes parked in one PARKED.md line. No S14 trigger: nothing ported.

Redesigned the I12 suite (redteam) around host-side evidence, since the product answers no guest frame and has no supervisor pid before a run: the payload, root in the guest, takes a duplicate of the init's channel fd by pidfd_getfd (peer vsock 2:5000) and writes forged frames, or opens fresh AF_VSOCK connections to 2:5000; the judge holds `workcell run` to ending within 15 s with exit 0, 125 or 126 as the kind allows, never a forged code, no panic and no control byte on stderr, and a plain run after. Eight tests, Bound 45 s: channel-malformed-frames (exit-empty, ack-short, version-2, unknown-type), channel-forged-exit-text (256 bytes of OSC, CSI, CR, NUL and a fake result line, must land as 126 with a clean stderr), channel-oversized-frame (4 GiB header then 40 s of silence), channel-slow-drip (one byte a second of valid Stdout frames), channel-connect-storm (32 fresh connections each carrying Exit 7), channel-connect-flood (768 fresh connections offering 256 KiB each, judged on host MemAvailable against a plain run with 64 MiB slack), channel-cut-mid-frame (half-close and full shutdown of the stolen fd mid-frame), channel-frames-after-exit. All eight FAIL on control (control_channel_linux.go replaces supervisor.go: a naive host that accepts and buffers everything, believes Exit frames, prints text raw and panics on short payloads); Session.Exec gives a judge the raw Output, and the workcell adapter passes exit 125 and 126 through. escape/enforced.txt gained I12 (T7).
The first hosted run showed seven passing on the product as it was and one real defect: the host left its vsock listener open after the init's connection, so the flood's 768 connections sat in the listener backlog and their bytes pinned 118 MiB of host memory beyond a plain run's 40 MiB for the whole session (I12, I10). Fix (builder, internal/vmm/firecracker/firecracker.go +16): Listen returns a listener whose first Accept closes the listener, which unlinks vsock_<port>, so Firecracker refuses every later guest connection; TestListenTakesOneConnection (no KVM) shows a queued connection reset, a later dial ENOENT and the first connection still carrying bytes. Hosted gate green on the next push: flood 10 MiB beyond a plain run's 34 MiB, all 20 enforced tests pass, gate 247 s, gate-macos 124 s, guard 7 s; local gate 14 s.
Review: no findings. Two reviewer notes and the redteam's unexpressed ideas are one line in PARKED.md. No S14 trigger: nothing ported.
Budget: product 6714 of 15000, test 13598 of 15071, WORKCELL_* 1 of 12, direct dependencies 3 of 15, flags 3 of 20, verbs 2 of 14.

Learned the hard way (first push red, second green): an *os.File on a FUSE file makes Go's runtime poller send FUSE_POLL from a thread holding its P, and the guest deadlocked against its own copier; the ADR rules that the init never touches its own mount through the os package, raw unix calls only. Before every (parent fd, name) call the node's whole ancestor chain is checked by fstatat AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW against the recorded dev and ino: the entry gone is ENOENT, an ancestor moved or replaced is ESTALE, healed by the kernel's next LOOKUP; RENAME holds the tree lock exclusively. No flag, verb, WORKCELL_* variable, module or Machine method; imports are the standard library, x/sys and internal/wire.

escape/ (redteam): sandbox.Config.Mount and suite.Want.Mount, forwarded as --mount after run; Want live; six mount-mode I1 rows (dotdot-traversal, planted-symlink, bind-mount, open-by-handle, device-node, link-rename) plus clone-mode link-rename with moveProbe (hard link of the secret into the export, a directory renamed out through .., a hard link out); one I10 row mount-disk-flood (Want cappedLive: --mount, 1 vCPU, 256 MiB, 64 MiB) that truncates a file in the export to 4 GiB, pwrites at its end and writes 128 MiB, judged by host free disk and the largest apparent size in the export. The real failure was in the init: on 1 vCPU (GOMAXPROCS=1) os.StartProcess vforks and the child's chdir into /workspace is a FUSE request that only the init's own copier can carry, but the forking thread keeps the only P, so the whole init froze and the host's stdin pump hit "wire: write frame: i/o timeout" (exit 125). Fix: mountFUSE sets GOMAXPROCS to at least 2 (cmd/workcell-init/mount.go, 8 lines), regression TestMount/capped (1 vCPU, 256 MiB, 64 MiB cap, 12 MiB of stdin through cat, truncate 4G, dd 128 MiB, sync, rm). Mount mode had only been run with 2 vCPUs before.

Accept not met, so W-802 is set back to [ ] with the reason at the end of its accept line, and W-802b is done: the run is read and the fallback of its accept line is carried out. Only loop/state/ changed.
Why repro is red: every go build and mkimage step exited 0; diff -u sums-a sums-b shows guest-amd64.img and guest-arm64.img equal across runs a and b, and workcell-linux-amd64, workcell-linux-arm64, workcell-init-amd64 and workcell-init-arm64 each different. Reproduced here on Go 1.27.0: two clean builds (fresh GOCACHE, -trimpath, the workflow's LDFLAGS) of cmd/workcell give the same hash with a clean tree, a different hash once an untracked sums-a lies in the checkout (go version -m shows vcs.modified true), and the same hash with and without that file under -buildvcs=false. The step's tee "sums-a" writes into the checkout before run b builds, so the VCS stamp, not the code, breaks reproducibility; the images carry no stamp and match.
Learned: Go stamps vcs.revision, vcs.time and vcs.modified into a main package built inside a git checkout even with -trimpath and -buildid=, so a reproducibility compare must build with -buildvcs=false or keep every scratch file outside the tree; the fuzz job's elapsed line reaches 10m0s before go test prints ok at 600 s, so the job's 621 to 644 s is build plus fuzz; the issue job's gh issue list --search found no earlier issue and created one, as W-802 intended; a nightly red run leaves no loop-halt label, so the loop is not halted by it.

## 2026-11-08T19:45Z H-806 DECIDED mode=maintainer
Optional gate done. Compared workcell-v2 against the open-source agent-sandbox field (Docker Sandboxes, E2B, Tencent CubeSandbox, OpenAI Codex CLI, Claude Code sandbox-runtime, gVisor, Kata) against I1-I14. Result is private in ~/workcell-h806/comparison.md and nowhere on GitHub, as the gate requires. An independent review pass corrected the draft (Codex has an opt-in allowlist proxy, not all-or-nothing; Docker Sandboxes is the closest analogue and was added; destination-bound credential injection is shared with Docker sbx/CubeEgress/iron-proxy, not unique; the two Claude srt bypasses are one CVE plus one non-CVE report, both already patched). Findings: workcell's least-matched invariants among those examined are I8 (safe return path) and I9 (guest-cannot-forge host-verifiable audit built into the sandbox); its proven, fail-closed, destination-bound credential path puts it among the very few. No NEW weakness was found in any other project or in released v1, so there is nothing to disclose privately and wait on before closing (the gate's precondition). Honest weaknesses recorded against workcell itself: the here-untested macOS backend, irreducible VMM trust, and a green suite being a test matrix not a proof of absence. Nothing weakness-bearing is written to this public repository.
Next: U-906 still waits on H-409, H-703 and a nightly run; H-806 no longer blocks Phase 10.
